From these LBAs the pedagogical academies with the substructure of musical-pedagogical high-level Gymnasia became the educational universities a few years ago. Such a growing system, which of course, because it was originally LBAs, i.e. secondary schools, has always been included in the class, cannot simply be eliminated from today to tomorrow. This system does a lot of good. On the other hand, the universities, which were always responsible for the training of the AHS teachers. This development dates back to the 18th century. Century, I could sketch it now, but not in two minutes. That is because there are these two traditions and because one has to work and expect with these two traditions, which have established themselves, great art is now required to bring these two institutions closer together, so that they may at some point be a uniform institution for good teacher training. And we do that with this law, which is now under review. We have made sure that this law makes teacher training much better first and second, that it is cooperative. So we have set the course towards more cooperation. In the end, may a track come out, on which the train of teacher training rides powerfully.

All this, that is simply more scientific at all levels, is to be guaranteed. The key body for this is a newly established Quality Assurance Council, which will be assigned to the same parts by the Ministry of Education and Science and will be filled with high-level international experts. And this Quality Assurance Council will see that the curriculum to be developed corresponds to the meaning of the law.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e11357	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I refer to the Administrative Jurisdiction Adjustment Act. With the novelle 2012, a new, two-stage administrative jurisdiction was created. The essential content is that the administrative court in the countries as well as two administrative courts are set up at the Federal Government, a Federal Administrative Court and a Federal Financial Court, and the independent administrative Senates of the countries as well as numerous other non-profit-making special authorities of the Federal Government are now dissolved and the administrative instance is essentially abolished. This essentially means that decisions issued by the authority of first instance can therefore only be challenged in the future at the administrative court. The administrative court of first instance will usually decide on the matter itself, and as another instance, only the administrative court will act more. This means that, in particular, the training and professional laws of the health professions are affected, in particular as regards the practice of the profession, as well as chamber and disciplinary law, as well as those professional groups and chambers. This is very, very dry now, but if one also looks at the opinions of the professional representatives, it is quite positive on the one hand that the introduction of a new system of administrative jurisdiction is welcomed, in particular by the accompanying strengthening of the rule of law. On the other hand, it is regretted that this administrative jurisdictional novelle, which is very extensive throughout the world, will mean a further weakening of self-government. The abolition of the administrative instance by the exclusion of the right to new administrative courts directly concerns the dental chamber and medical chamber self-administration, for example in the field of disciplinary law by the withdrawal of the disciplinary board, the collection of chamber contributions, by the absence of the Federal Committee as an appeal body or in the field of the welfare fund by the resolution of the appeal committee. It is to be feared that this system change will lead to a fragmentation of administrative practice by uneven judicial decisions. [[President Dr. Graf takes over the chairmanship.]] Many unrounded stories were included in the original bill, which were erased according to the opinions in the treatment. Apart from very few small points, we are satisfied with these things and can therefore agree to this law. One thing I would like to mention now: As I said in the committee, it was a bit strange for my concept of democracy to say that in the original law you were accused of the following – in the way: Let's see if it's going through! –, I just read it: In paragraph 5, a comprehensive right of appeal or revision against the decision of the Federal Arbitration Commission and the Federal Administrative Court is granted to the Federal Minister for Health. - Quote. If that were to happen, it would have been something that is, of course, extremely worrying in terms of democracy and of which it is not actually Usus to advocate it in laws, because that is contrary to the principles of the division of powers. This would give a executing authority, which is not a party to a administrative or administrative procedure, the right to combat a decision. We are glad that these things are not inside, so we can vote here. As I said, we're going to give it our yes.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e11796	Mr President. Minister! High house! Our request for reform of the school system is based on a report of the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors has found that there are double-tracks in the competences between the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Education that would be eliminated. The Federal Government is further responsible for the school doctors of the Federal Schools, and for the school doctors in compulsory schools, the country and the municipalities are responsible as school holders. This leads to a strong inhomogeneity of the school's services as a whole. In order to increase the efficiency of this school health service, the strict separation between school health care and health care should be removed. The school doctors carry out a number of studies each year. General data such as the size, age and weight of the respective student, as well as information on allergies, vision, teeth, neck, nose, ear diseases, respiratory diseases and behavioural damage are included. This is all recorded in health papers, but it is already done, because they are neither electronically recorded nor passed on to the Federal Ministry of Health. These data cannot be evaluated for health policy purposes either. As a result, there are no grounds for identifying risk groups or problem areas, and no preventive measures can be derived from them. In order to increase the efficiency and meaningfulness of these studies, we have tabled this proposal, which is intended to produce such an overall report and to use the data collected, anonymized of course, for health policy evaluations. If I leave the committee after the vote, this request will be rejected again. I am nevertheless pleased that these demands will be implemented, since they are a recommendation from the Court of Auditors.

In this abundance of tasks, which they would actually have to carry out in the course of control, if they were to perceive them wisely, they could not do the control in this form. It was our concern to move forward. The government parties were of the opinion that we do not need that, and we reject it. We urge you to give more attention to all parties – it will not be possible in this legislative period anyway – in the next legislative period from the outset, to pay more attention to it and to ensure that the control structures that are urgently necessary and urgently need better coordination are also better equipped.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e14388	On a point of view, Mrs. Federal Minister Dr. Fekter on the floor. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e14412	Madam President. High house! Values listeners! With the written parliamentary request of 6. Mr Podgorschek, Mr President-in-Office of the Council. — (NL) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of the Council and the President-in-Office of the Council for his excellent report. Of course, I take my duty to speak and answer you as Minister of Finance on all matters of execution by the Federal Ministry of Finance, very seriously. I was a member of the House myself for years, and transparency is not just a keyword for me. However, I must also pay attention to where, ladies and gentlemen, you, the House itself, have set me as a Constitutional Legislator limits for a reply. In accordance with Article 52 B-VG, the National Council is empowered to review the management of the Federal Government, to consult its members on all matters of enforcement and to request all relevant information. These control rights to the Federal Government and its members also exist in respect of undertakings in which the Federal Government is involved, with at least 50 percent of its capital, and which are subject to the audit of the Court of Auditors. § 90 2. The sentence of the Rules of Procedure specifies the subject matter of enforcement, i.e. the subject-matter of the case law, using the wording of Section 2 para. 3 of the Federal Ministry Act. Accordingly, government acts, administrative matters or the administration of the federal government are to be understood as bodies of private rights. It follows from the above-mentioned legal basis that matters of a different legal person, as such, cannot constitute the subject-matter of parliamentary questions, but only in so far as an activity of the institutions of the Federal Government refers to such a legal person. There is no administrative activity required for parliamentary control within the meaning of the B-VG where an independent legal entity acts in a private legal form. The possibility of interpolation begins where management powers or ingenuity of the members of the government are used in relation to the activities of the independent legal entity. [[Abg. Brosz: motion for a resolution by the Minister!]] The OeNB is a public limited company to which the provisions of the Stock Corporation are to be applied, as far as the General European Treaty, the Statute of the ESCB/ECB or this Federal Act are concerned. – This is stated in § 1 of the Banking Act. The Board therefore has to manage the OeNB under its own responsibility, as the company's well-being, taking into account the interests of shareholders and employees and the public interest, in so far as there are no explicit statutory mandates of state bodies. Due to these limited inconsistencies of the Federal Minister of Finance or the Federal Ministry of Finance on the OeNB – namely the protection of OeNB's independence – the OeNB's activities can only be subject to an interpellation insofar as the OeNB is highly active and is also bound by instructions from a minister – for example, § 15 Foreign Exchange Act – not against it when it comes to the own-responsible management of the OeNB. If the OeNB acts in private law, there shall be no administrative activity necessary for parliamentary control within the meaning of our Constitution. This is all the more true of OeNB subsidiaries, such as the Company Licensing Management GmbH, which manages the apartments that Mr Podgorschek spoke about. The management of OeNB's housing or housing is clearly private-law action by the OeNB or its subsidiary. Consequently, there is no administrative activity within the meaning of the B-VG, and therefore I am not entitled to provide information here under constitutional law. I am sorry that I am not able to contribute to the brightening of the cases discussed here today. [[Interim calls at BZÖ and FPÖ.]] However, I should like to draw attention to the fact that the Financial Committee has a long tradition, namely that the OeNB institutions are invited to speak and answer there. [[Abg. Grosz: But you can do something about the letter...!]] And since Governor Novotny himself had been a member of the House for several years, he always accepted these invitations to a current debate with the OeNB personally and has already answered questions there which I could not answer as a minister in the inter-pellation procedure. I therefore ask the House to use this instrument and to ask the questions directly to the Governor himself. With regard to the suggestion that I should call on the Court of Auditors, I would point out that the Court of Auditors is an institution of Parliament. And therefore, ladies and gentlemen, it would be easy: You yourself could contact the Court of Auditors on this matter. Thank you.

That is why we see this with great scepticism and will also make a corresponding amendment in this respect. What's the reason? – There are various findings of the Constitutional Court, which also concern this matter directly and indirectly, and there is also strong criticism of the Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellor's Office that this novelle is unconstitutional. Why? – On the one hand, it is not understandable that, in the case of temporary changes to a plant, with the exception of protection against the risk of life and health, the remaining protection interests under § 74 para. 2 of the Trade Regulation 1994 must not be observed. This is about protecting against smell, noise, emissions and vibrations. Another point is: There is no factual justification for the exemption of approval and thus the privilege of non-emission-neutral changes in an operating plant, if this is only temporary. These are two essential aspects that we see very skeptical, especially for those who are on board. We Greens are a member of the platform for work-free Sunday, so we believe that this can be a possible small step forward. In general, I believe, Mr Minister, that this debate must be dealt with very in principle and in detail so that there is no need for occasions in order to find an appropriate legal system.

It must be predicted that the commercial regulation dates back to 1858, and that the reason for it was that one has on the one hand regulated how the rights of scale and the rights of exercise for the commercial operators look, and on the other hand has also developed the right of investment. Why? – Because what has been done here, of course, has also been involved in the environment and partly in the neighbourhood area. Of course, there is always a field of tension, which is not easy to highlight by the fact that you can only disassemble on one side and not take the other side into account. It is precisely because there have been several occasions here in connection with operational transfers – but also with deregulation proposals from the countries – that we have, above all, tried to make progress in the field of investment. I believe that this has succeeded without restricting the rights of neighborhoods. I should like to point out the following: As far as plant law is concerned, we now have a new service for entrepreneurs. At the request of the Authority, all decisions and conditions relating to a holding are now to be compiled. This prevents bad surprises during takeovers, because the taker was often confronted with conditions only after the transfer and then the joy in handing over was no longer the one she might have been. In particular, a concentrated processing process was not possible. Since the tourism conference took place yesterday, I should also like to point out that we have just found a form of financing for contractors, which, when investing, will free the purchasers of ten years of interest. Although interest rates are currently low, this is a significant improvement. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Hörl.]] – We have done this for Mr Hörl and his members. [[==References====External links== Dr. Matznetter.]] Secondly, I should like to refer to a simplification of local competence, since many companies operate in several countries, federal states. There is a clear definition and consideration of how jurisdiction disputes will be settled in the future. Thirdly, we - and I see this as further - have the correction of excessive requirements. Technology has partly changed, the opportunities have changed, and if someone here not only experiences intensifications, but also reliefs, on request, without questioning the rights of neighborhoods, then we clearly see this as progress, and many of those concerned detto. We have also granted approval to public viewing. Mrs Lichtenecker, I have now heard your amendment. In itself - I think Mr Matznetter or someone else has already pointed this out - we have actually cleared this up in committee. Of course, there has been criticism from the BKA's Constitutional Service, but we have just cleared these criticisms: To ensure that no one is automatically confronted with public viewing in his or her neighboring garden, the regulation applies to major events that have been defined for this purpose. That is, in the final effect, this is an event that has a broad consensus that there is an interest in it. That is why it is free of authorisation and limited to a short period of time. That is why I would not see the problems outlined in the amendment. I'd rather if you would like to withdraw the request, but I can't prescribe anything. I do not think he is entitled from the point of view. Last but not least – and here the circle now closes and goes in the direction of the amendment tabled by Mr Katzian – is also a part of the trade order, which regulates the scope of an activity. In concrete terms, we are dealing with the rights of minors. One can discuss everything, but I do not think that anyone who has a clear intention is dealing with it. This is the case in connection with the shop opening. I am always asked two questions in connection with ‘dayli’. – First question: Isn't that some kind of event legislation? – I would say, in fact, it is not, because we have the intention that the Sunday opening should not be general, also We had already had this in the past, and we had regulated it accordingly. What we're doing now is just a clarification. Second question: If this does not prevent something from happening, will it not give more opportunities to all operators? – I tell you, the experience shows you are no longer making purchases with openings. The purchasing power only shifts from the little ones to the big ones. This shift is quite simple. Why? – If one starts, everyone else will follow. Therefore, the following When “dayli” starts, all the big chains also come in because they have to. Who has the disadvantage? – The businessman who is not able to dispossess with the employees who himself is in the business or still has to open up with a few employees. We do not want this shift in the market, this structural change from small to large. This is not fair competition. Secondly, ladies and gentlemen: If you look at existing possibilities regarding Sunday, if they are all exploited, then I'd say: What goes on in tourism, yes, except for the city of Vienna. There seems to be no need there. This must be decided by the city of Vienna with its traders themselves. On the other hand, during the loading hours under the week, the frame is not even exploited. That is why I think it is difficult to deduce here, someone – we or Parliament – would oppose the free market. Every market needs certain rules. I also consider it an act of self-understanding: If Parliament sets out a rule, others should not ignore this rule. That is why I am also grateful for the cooperation of almost all the parties that this seal was possible here. The legal quality can be discussed, because this effort was naturally carried out by the effort to include everything, but I believe that in general this has also been achieved. That is why I also thank you for this clear regulation.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e19721	Mr President. Estimated Minister of State! High house! Today, we decide on the constitutional adaptation of individual laws relating to the Ministry of Economic Affairs to administrative jurisdiction. The basis for these necessary changes is the decision on the two-stage administrative jurisdiction of May last year. As you know, at the beginning of 2014, an administrative court in the countries and two administrative courts will be set up at the Federal Government. More than 120 special authorities will be dissolved at one time. The introduction of this two-stage system is the largest reform of the administrative organisation in the Federal Republic of Germany and countries since 1920. The associated structural reforms are an important contribution to administrative simplification and administrative reform. As far as this Act of Adjustment is concerned, I can stress that it only makes technical changes to the various legislation on matter. We do not make any material changes to the law, even though this, as it is now, has been proposed by some. It is therefore merely a matter of constitutional adaptation to administrative jurisdiction and not of substantive revisions. I'm allowed to ask for your consent. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e19755	Next speaker: Mr Windholz. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e19776	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Minister! High house! I can actually make it short. The Administrative Jurisdiction Adjustment laws, which are now in place, constitute administrative simplification of the principle. At the time of the introduction, however, we also said critically that we see the increase in administrative penalties and in individual areas the increase in procedures rather critically, but if we consider all areas, we will agree to these changes of law.

Point Report of the Constitutional Committee on the Government Template (2168 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Act amending the Data Protection Act 2000 (DSG-Novelle 2014) (2268 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e19963	We're coming to 19. Agenda item. The debate opens up to Mr Wittmann. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e19987	Mr President. High house! Mr. State Secretary! This Federal Act amends the Data Protection Act 2000. The essential part of this innovation is that a data protection authority should be set up to replace the Data Protection Commission. It is said to be much more independent and to move further away from the Federal Chancellery. It is intended to be a monocratically established authority, which is essentially based – not entirely – on the organizational form of the public prosecutors. There is a Head of Service appointed by the Federal President on the proposal of the Federal Chancellor. The Service Authority is its own staff. The staff of the Data Protection Authority itself are therefore only subject to the instructions of the head of the Data Protection Authority. In essence, there is only a very far-reaching right of information of the Federal Chancellor, and this right of information can be interpreted by the Director of the Data Protection Authority in such a way that any suspicion of non-independence results in him not exercising or refusing to exercise this right of information. The whole of this is the result of a judgment that has instructed us to give greater independence to the new data protection authority, because the European Court of Justice considered that the settlement at the Federal Chancellor's Office itself does not guarantee independence as transparent as we do now. I believe that this is a very essential step in the sense of the tasks that this data protection authority receives. Perhaps it is worth mentioning that the Data Protection Authority can also consult and ultimately deliver opinions on matters of importance. A good step towards independence of data protection, which is becoming increasingly essential and more important.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e20021	Next speaker: Mr Mag. Lettenbichler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e20044	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! This also originates in the decision of the National Council of May this year when we decided on the two-stage administrative jurisdiction. ==References== In January 2014, many federal and national authorities are abandoned, thus speeding up and simplifying procedures for citizens. The related valuable contribution to the administrative reform has already been discussed several times in detail at these two plenary sessions. The structural reform in the administration, which was also addressed by Mr Wittmann, is also linked to the resolution of the Data Protection Commission, which could, however, cause Austria EU-law problems. Because of the EU Data Protection Directive, we are obliged to set up a control body to verify compliance with data protection rules. And for this reason, the Data Protection Commission is abandoned, but replaced by a Data Protection Authority. This is led by a head appointed for five years. Decisions of the Data Protection Authority may be challenged by the Federal Administrative Court. Decisions are to be taken by a Senate involving competent lay judges. I'm allowed to ask for your consent. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e20074	Next speaker: Mr Herbert. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e20096	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) As my previous speakers have pointed out, the reason for the 2014 Data Protection Act Novelle is a directive of the EU that has just stated that the old Data Protection Commission, which was under the Federal Chancellor's Office and was also obliged to report to the Chancellor, now no longer has a legal right to exist in this new Administrative Jurisdiction, which is why this government template now creates a new data protection authority, which will be an independent authority with its own personal sovereignty and independent authority structure and will now also comply with its personal and content composition of this Data Protection Directive. For us, however, it was important that we not only change the labels, that we not only introduce new terms, but also that in terms of content some adjustments be made, a further development of this data protection law, which we have also succeeded. And I should like to thank both the government parties and the speakers of my club, who, in good negotiations, were able to reach some essential points of innovation in the Data Protection Act. I can only mention here that the initial Advisory Board, which was originally set up in the first draft, is now being removed without replacement and that the Data Protection Council is being upgraded as an advisory body. He may now also draw up opinions or order them. And I also do not want to mention the annual report to be addressed to the Federal Chancellor and will now also be available to Parliament, which will enable a transparent assessment of the work of the new data protection authority. It is also particularly important for me to point out the Committee's position, since there may be a margin for interpretation of the party's position and the possibility of appeal within the framework of the federal court. So not only contracting authorities of public law, but of course – and this is reflected in this committee statement – private clients are now entitled, to use this maximum jurisdiction or to refer to the highest courts. And the basic problem is as follows: There are more and more tasks, but constant staff. I have looked at the tasks we have decided here in Parliament alone for the data protection authority not too long ago. That was the storage of stock data, the transparency database, the security police law Novelle 2011, the TKG-Novelle 2011, ELGA, all the projects that we can remember very well, because they are just one, two years behind, all the novels that provide new tasks for the data protection authority. There was no more staff. The data protection authority, if properly informed, has just received a service in the last ten years. More tasks, but no more staff means nothing more than that you can do less and less and that data protection on the control level of course gets a problem in the medium term. And if you read the data protection report carefully, you can see that the Data Protection Commission is old, the Data Protection Authority is talking about the problems quite openly. It says that the Ombudsman procedure will no longer be possible in the six months envisaged. Or, in the case of official control procedures, it is said that one can no longer control to the desired extent. As far as data storage is concerned, the data protection authority should in fact control the provider whether they comply with the deletion obligations or whether they are stored under the statutory framework conditions. To date, my information has not yet been controlled by a provider, not because the data protection authority would not do so, but it does not have the staff and it does not have the necessary personnel for it, because it requires technical employees who have the know-how. And even in the data protection report is quite open inside, one does not have these employees with the necessary technical know-how, because there are no services necessary for that. That's an unpleasant situation. The Secretary of State always says, well, on the one hand, we need to save in the administration and on the other hand more staff is required. Now it is true that one does not need to build up administration where it is not necessary. But I think it is obvious that there is an increasing need for more staff in the case of technological development, which is becoming increasingly rapid. This is not a question of waste of resources in the area of administration. If you look at how international data protection authorities are equipped, and the Austrian data protection authority, then everything becomes clear. In Austria, there are 21 plan posts with the Data Protection Authority, in comparable countries such as Belgium and Sweden twice as many. It can't go well. If we do not change this situation, Mr State Secretary, if you no longer negotiate, then this will lead to a super-GAU data protection law in the medium term. We don't want to. We agree, but it won't be enough to exchange the door sign. They will need to get involved in the next negotiations to get more personnel for the data protection authority. - Thank you.

Due to the time pressure due to the delivery of the Eurofighters, the reconstruction work had to be carried out very intensively there. There was a lack of planning depth in the project. This led to delays and thus to these increases in the construction budget. Many improvements and recommendations from the Court of Auditors have already been implemented internally as a result of the internal revision and the Commission of Inquiry. On the one hand, there is a new directive on military construction, which has been applicable since 1. ==References== The redesign of the in-house project management has also been put on new feet in order to manage and plan the efficient execution of large construction projects well. The construction administration was also reorganised by the state defence department, and the four-eye principle was further introduced in various interests. Procedures based on cost and profit analysis have also been introduced. This report is again a good example of the fact that cooperation with the administration and the Court of Auditors leads to very successful implementation.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e21686	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The so-called visa scandal or the From 2003 to 2005, Visa-Affaire has hit high waves in Austria. This resulted in the establishment in 2005 of the so-called Independent Visa Commission, which set out the requirements for the future creation of visas. Overall, a huge image damage has been caused to the country. Staff of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs were also sentenced. But, of course, there were also many people who were drawn flat-rate in miscredit. In March and June 2010, the Court of Auditors reviewed the measures the Visa Commission has imposed and also carried out on-the-spot inspections, in Kiev and Moscow, in the most powerful messages on visas. There are four main criticisms in total. The cooperation between the Federal Ministry of European and International Affairs and the Federal Ministry of the Interior was partly inadequate and in need of improvement. There was no comprehensive documentation, and the quality of the work processes was also lacking. The demarcation between service and professional supervision has not worked, even with the software has not been updated continuously and adjusted to the day needs, and so there have been loopholes. The four-eye principle, which would have been absolutely necessary, was also critically examined by the Court of Auditors. The recommendations are as follows: Optimization of logging and documentation, introduction of a mandatory rotation system for local embassies, clear admission and selection criteria for employees working in the visa field, clarification of technical supervision in order to avoid unexplained responsibilities and competences between the Federal Ministry of European and International Affairs and BMI, ongoing update of the database. A total of 32 recommendations were made by the Court of Auditors, 31 recommendations were met, and this was also welcomed by the president of the Committee. In Austria, more than 300 000 visa applications are currently being submitted, of which approximately 10,000 are rejected, but overall, it can be said that this Court of Auditors report and, of course, the Visa Commission have contributed to making the system more transparent and secure and reducing waiting times for the visas. It has also helped to improve the service. There are currently 39 offices exhibiting visas. For the future, it is planned to increase the number to 50. It was optimised the administration, the demarcation between the individual ministries. All in all, I think, you can say: It has succeeded in improving quality and transparency by the Visa Commission, which is currently resting, and the Court of Auditors report. I think that is in the sense of all those concerned who are applying for a visa today. Thank you.

It has been criticised that, at local level, the commitments to implement a medium-term budget are different from one state to another. It is also true that the quality of the medium-term budget varies depending on the federal state and also within the federal states depending on the municipality. Most of the municipalities, for example, transmit their planning data not electronically, but via the survey form. This leads to additional effort and additional costs. There would be our proposal – and this should and could be possible in the future – that these data be transmitted electronically. It is, of course, up to the representatives of the countries and municipalities to ensure that overall coordination of financial planning between different levels of administration is improved. Only then, from my point of view, can there be an even better and more accurate financial planning for Austria. I too would like to thank the Court of Auditors for the high-quality reports.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22304	Next speaker: Mrs Gessl-Ranftl. 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22326	Mr President. Mr President-in-Office of the Council! High house! In 2011, the Court of Auditors examined the lessons learned by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Ministry of the Interior from the so-called Visa Office in 2005 and what steps have already been taken. We have already discussed this intensively with State Secretary Lopatka in committee – and this has been mentioned several times today. The Court of Auditors 32 recommendations have been made, as Mr President-in-Office of the Council said, 31 of them have already been implemented. A clear arrangement between the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Ministry of Interior has been established since November 2011, and thus there is a safe, more efficient and transparent allocation of visas. However, I still have concerns that the four-eye principle, which was also recommended by the Court of Auditors, cannot be fully respected. I am particularly referring to staff resources. Staff resources are scarce. And if, as in Moscow, up to 2,000 visas are issued daily, the four-eye principle cannot be consistently observed. But I very much hope that more controls will be carried out due to this situation. It should be noted that enhanced cooperation with the local Schengen partners has been implemented at the various services. I am particularly addressing the Austrian Embassy in Kiev and Moscow. Finally, I would like to say that it is important for the system to be further improved to prevent and exclude abuse in the future. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22371	The next item is the speech by Mr Singer. 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22392	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I too would like to deal with the report by the Court of Auditors on consular matters, particularly the consequences for the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. It has already been mentioned that the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has implemented to a large extent the outstanding recommendations of the Visa Commission on the prevention of abuse in the issuing of visas. The 32 recommendations of the Court of Auditors have also been largely implemented. This is important and also the basis for starting today from a secure, transparent and efficient visa award. I should like to mention a few points which the Foreign Ministry has made as a result. On the one hand, they were structural measures, and in concrete terms their own consular building was rented in Moscow. We need to know that in Moscow about one third of all Austrian visa applications are handled. In Kiev, on the one hand, there were human consequences for disciplinary reasons, and on the other hand, the internal redeployment of the workforce could increase accordingly and thus also a claim of the Court of Auditors could be implemented. Interestingly, there is also cooperation with Schengen partners when it comes to issuing relevant visa applications. It should be noted that Austria is represented as an exhibition authority by 95 Schengen partners. Conversely, Austria represented 38 other Schengen partners. A corresponding computer has been introduced. I would just like to address the EU Visa Information System. And what is quite, very important, is the fact that there are also regular controls. In summary, ladies and gentlemen, from today's point of view, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has learned from the mistakes of the past, one can now talk about a crisis-proof visa system and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22435	Next speaker: Mr Hell. 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-04-26-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00200_d7e22456	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Why have most previous speakers dealt with the subject of “consular beings ”? – Because it has attracted high attention in the media when irregularities in the granting of visas have occurred and the public now wants to know whether improvements have been made in the procedures. The aim of the audit procedure was for the Court of Auditors to review the consular system and the implementation of the recommendations made by the Visa Commission in 2005. At that time, there were more violations and irregularities. Ten persons were sentenced to justice at the time. At the time, the Court found that, in particular, the insufficient clarifications of service and professional supervision between ministries have led to unclear responsibilities and lack of controls. The debate in the Court of Auditors, but also in the report itself, shows that most of the recommendations have now been implemented and weaknesses between the ministries have been addressed. The four-eye principle required by the Commission, and this point has already been raised several times today, has not been sufficiently implemented according to the Court of Auditors' report. State Secretary Lopatka has pointed out in the Committee that there are now substantial improvements in controls, but full implementation is not yet organisationally possible due to the volume of work, but a follow-up evaluation will take place. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! With the implementation of the recommendation of the Visa Commission, the granting of visas in Austrian representations is now safer, more transparent and more efficient. I would also like to thank the Court of Auditors for its work which has contributed to these improvements.
